## 2026-11-23 — Stronger pastel status pills added to the authorized repair batch

Owner adds all pill accent colors to the same repair/deployment request: keep pastel appearance and black outlines, make colors more distinctive, and verify semantic conventions including amber versus orange. Official Carbon status guidance supports blue information/progress, yellow/amber attention, orange more serious warning/escalation, red error, green success, gray neutral (https://www.carbondesignsystem.com/building-blocks/core/patterns/status-indicators). GOV.UK tags supports consistent labeled light-background/dark-text tags (https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/tag/). W3C requires meaning beyond color and sufficient text contrast, not one prescribed palette (https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/use-of-color.html; https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/contrast-minimum.html). Exact stronger Lucid tones are owner-authorized design choices; do not call them a W3C-mandated palette.

## 2026-11-22 — Native-style mobile planning direction; no framework migration

Owner pauses the manual walkthrough because clipped work areas/instructions prevent meaningful testing. He requests a conceptual React Native engineering plan for Lucid, then translation of that screen organization and interaction model into the existing Laravel web views, not a React Native rebuild or dependency migration. React Native is a candidate planning vocabulary, not a unique visual design standard and not a claim about comparative model training. Platform-native guidance (Android adaptive layouts/Material and Apple HIG) and W3C web requirements remain the design authorities; Flutter's responsive/adaptive guidance was also reviewed as a valid alternative reference. No choice of accordion, bottom navigation, drawer, sheet or full-screen help is silently approved.

Planning sequence: reproduce the current shared shells at narrow/tall and wide/short phone sizes and tablet sizes; inventory screen/task/navigation/guide/source/draft states; design the hypothetical native screen map with clear Home/Back, task-first usable content, reachable keyboard-aware composer and purpose-appropriate help/source views; map each interaction back to semantic Blade/Livewire/Flux and browser layout behavior; present the proposed mobile/tablet layout and tradeoffs before implementation, preserving approved desktop behavior unless separately changed. Include Customer support, private documents, invoices, policies, Agent desk and Ask Lucid rather than fixing one shell only. Verify normal/loading/error/streaming/Stop/reset, keyboard/rotation/text size and continuity/privacy. Browser emulation supports layout reproduction; it does not prove real Android/iOS keyboard, microphone, clipboard or browser-toolbar behavior.

Official references consulted: [React Native core components](https://reactnative.dev/docs/components-and-apis), [ScrollView](https://reactnative.dev/docs/scrollview), [KeyboardAvoidingView](https://reactnative.dev/docs/keyboardavoidingview), [Android layout basics](https://developer.android.com/design/ui/mobile/guides/layout-and-content/layout-basics), [Android canonical adaptive layouts](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/layouts/adaptive/canonical-layouts), [Apple HIG layout](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/layout), and [Flutter adaptive/responsive guidance](https://docs.flutter.dev/ui/adaptive-responsive), alongside W3C/GOV.UK sources below. Reference frameworks are not installed/introduced. Source inspection confirms shared explicit height/min-height/overflow and stacked-control rules to investigate, not a browser-reproduced root cause. No implementation, new dependency, test run, commit or deployment for this planning checkpoint.

## 2026-11-22 — Mobile layout research required; closing instructions does not free workspace

Samsung Galaxy A54 portrait, Customer support: Terry reports Customer chat/Create support ticket controls stacked above the work area, instructions underneath, and the same clipped question-button area after tapping the instructions X. Show instructions then appears at the top, but the usable work area does not increase. Closing instructions therefore did not resolve the reported space/clipping problem. This is an owner observation, not an established CSS/grid/viewport root cause. No screenshot is required to retain the finding; inspect/reproduce the affected page and scroll boundaries before selecting a repair.

Owner requires a carefully researched responsive-web design decision using primary standards and established official design-system guidance, with the same rigor used for Laravel APIs. Accordions/dropdowns are suggestions to evaluate, not an approved implementation. Compare ordinary document flow/reflow, simpler visible content, a single optional-help disclosure, multiple-section accordions, task/navigation alternatives and a separate help view where appropriate. Explain tradeoffs and any documentation gap before choosing; do not adopt the first familiar pattern or compress essential chat/ticket controls to preserve desktop geometry. Preserve approved desktop behavior and inspect both phone orientations and tablet states. This is a recorded future repair requirement; no UI/code changes, tests, commit or deployment in this note.

Primary research completed for this checkpoint: [W3C WCAG 2.2 Reflow](https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/#reflow), [Orientation](https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/#orientation), [Focus Not Obscured](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/focus-not-obscured-minimum.html), [Target Size Minimum](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/target-size-minimum.html), WAI-ARIA APG [Disclosure](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/disclosure/) and [Accordion](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/accordion/), and official GOV.UK Design System [Accordion](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/accordion/), [Details](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/details/) and [Tabs](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/tabs/). WCAG is the normative accessibility baseline; Understanding/APG explain criteria/pattern implementation, and GOV.UK is evidence-based system-specific design guidance rather than a universal W3C requirement.

Findings to apply during design review: non-excepted content must reflow without losing information/functionality under the criterion's conditions; both orientations need support unless an orientation is essential. Keyboard focus must not be entirely obscured by author-created content; target sizing/spacing has specified exceptions, not an unconditional native-app pixel rule. Disclosure describes one show/hide section; accordion covers multiple interactive sections and requires correct control/state semantics. GOV.UK advises testing simpler visible content first, not hiding information everyone needs, not nesting accordions, and considering a single Details disclosure for one optional section. Its Tabs implementation linearizes on small screens, so simply stacking desktop task tabs is not automatically the right mobile design. These sources do not mandate an accordion, prescribe Lucid's exact layout, or prove a root cause for the reported clipping. Reclaiming workspace when help closes is an owner layout goal, not itself a quoted WCAG requirement. No component choice is settled by this research alone.

## 2026-11-22 — Blade formatter compatibility investigation; decision pending

Terry paused the manual walkthrough to request a complete compatibility investigation before deciding whether to enable Blade formatting. No application formatter setting, dependencies, templates, CI configuration or runtime behavior was changed. Recommendation: adopt template formatting as a separate tooling batch before resuming final acceptance, but do not simply enable the current Pint Blade rule as-is. The mixed stack is supported; the unresolved concern is configuration and validation, not a prohibition on Flux/Livewire/Alpine.

Verified installed Pint 1.30.5 and its PHAR implementation, not assumptions based on PHP-only older versions. Blade is opt-in through --blade or Pint/laravel_blade. It uses Prettier plus unscoped prettier-plugin-blade (Forte/Chisel, not Shufo's separately scoped plugin) and prettier-plugin-tailwindcss. Chisel explicitly includes flux/livewire component prefixes. Pint handles embedded PHP itself, masks mixed Blade/script content and Alpine patterns, and skips designated email/guideline files. This does not format standalone resources/js or CSS files; those require their own formatter scope. Our views currently have Alpine attributes and Livewire single-file PHP, but no literal script blocks to exercise. Documentation does not promise arbitrary mixed-template correctness or identical visual rendering.

Isolated trial dependencies: Prettier 3.10.1, Blade plugin 3.3.3 and Tailwind plugin 0.8.1, satisfying Pint's declared ranges. Local Node 22.23.3 meets the strictest dependency requirement (20.19+). Isolated npm audit reported zero published advisories; this is not a security guarantee. Actual adoption must install approved development dependencies with a lockfile, keep one formatter responsible per file type and prevent conflicting editor formatting. CI currently runs Pint before Node/npm installation; move those installation steps before Blade-enabled Pint. Do not rely on an interactive dependency-install prompt in CI.

Important Tailwind 4 limitation: official Tailwind plugin guidance requires tailwindStylesheet pointing to the app CSS entry for theme/custom-utility-aware sorting. Pint 1.30.5's bundled configuration omits it, and resources/js/worker.cjs directly uses bundled options without resolving project Prettier configuration. Adding a root .prettierrc alone does not fix that integration. Decide between accepting Pint's default sorting with this explicit limitation, a documented upstream configuration solution, or a separately configured Blade/Prettier command while Pint continues PHP formatting. Do not patch vendor files or run competing Blade formatters. The separately configured option must also verify embedded PHP coverage; the standalone plugin's optional PHP formatter is not the same integration as Pint's PHP handling.

Trial covered copies of all 69 owned Blade views, including the Flux navlist override, settings single-file components and large Alpine expressions. First pass changed 61; two required a second pass, then the stability check passed. All 69 originals and 69 formatted copies compiled and PHP-linted without errors. Tag sequences and static class token sets matched. A JavaScript probe parsed 320 expressions: 319 canonical outputs matched, and the remaining difference was manually reviewed as equivalent wrapping/trailing commas. Another 29 collected values were PHP bindings rather than parseable JavaScript and were unchanged. Mixed Blade values were masked for JS parsing, so this probe is not a proof of PHP expression equivalence or all future syntax.

Functional trial in an isolated app copy: formatted views passed 73/78 selected feature tests (490 assertions). Fresh unformatted controls passed 76/78 (513 assertions): the same two document/indexing-related failures remained, while formatting introduced three raw-whitespace text assertion failures in LucidShellTest. Their copy remains present with changed line breaks; update assertions to meaningful normalized/rendered text rather than weakening behavior coverage if adopted. Browser trial passed 23/27 cases (251 assertions), with three optional skips and one recording-navigation timeout. The navigation timeout reproduced with fresh unformatted views, so it is not established as a formatter regression. Initial temporary-app routing/package setup failures and a stale compiled-view control were discarded; final control results above use cleared temporary view caches. These trials do not establish a clean full suite, Linux CI, desktop/phone visual equivalence or hosted acceptance. The actual app's preceding 1,078-pass gate remains unchanged; it was not a Blade-enabled gate.

Before claiming adoption complete: settle the Tailwind configuration approach; approve/install pinned development dependencies; configure editor/local/CI consistently; review the formatting-only diff and stable second run; adjust whitespace assertions without losing coverage; inspect rendered desktop/phone controls, bubbles, sources, menus, settings, forms and whitespace-sensitive content; run the required final composer gate and CI. Keep this separate from behavior fixes so any regression is attributable. ## 2026-11-21 — Send during recording preference and documented patterns

Terry dislikes having to Stop before the Send arrow becomes available and asks which behavior is an industry standard, while expressing flexibility. Official OpenAI dictation guidance describes transcription into editable text before Send (https://help.openai.com/en/articles/12168547-voice-dictation-faq). Google Gboard advanced voice typing documents a Send voice command and a mode for sending multiple messages with the microphone on (https://support.google.com/gboard/answer/11197787?hl=en). These are different supported product patterns, not evidence of a universal mandatory stop-before-send standard; no such standard was established by this search.

Recommendation for owner consideration: keep Stop for finalize-and-review, but allow an explicit Send click during recording to stop capture, await final transcription and submit exactly once. Do not send interim text, keep capture running after submission, restore sent phrases, or auto-submit after a transcription failure; retain the draft for correction on failure. This would remove the extra required Stop click while keeping finalization boundaries. Current implementation still blocks Send during capture/finalization; no code or tests changed and this recommendation is not yet recorded as an approved implementation change.

## 2026-11-21 — background focus versus chat navigation clarified

Official documentation checked: https://help.openai.com/en/articles/12168547-voice-dictation-faq describes dictation as recorded audio returned as editable text before Send; it does not specify whether desktop dictation continues when another app has focus. https://help.openai.com/en/articles/20001274-chatgpt-voice documents background conversations under Settings > Voice, including other apps/locked phones, for Voice conversations; that is not documentation of desktop dictation behavior. Do not claim the latter from the former or imply the assistant can observe the owner's live capture state.

Recommended Lucid behavior, pending owner confirmation: distinguish temporary app/tab focus changes from navigating to another Lucid chat. Leaving focus alone can leave an explicitly started recording attached to its original composer, with clear recording indication and existing duration bound, subject to browser/OS limitations. Navigating to another Lucid chat should stop/finalize capture, retain the unsent draft in its original chat, never transfer it, and return with microphone off until explicitly restarted. Current tested navigation stops capture and prevents leakage but returns a blank composer; that establishes only those two safety outcomes, not complete desired draft retention. Background-focus behavior has not been tested or accepted. No implementation changes.

A contextual first request with no prior subject asks a brief clarification. New named-topic questions remain independent; corpus isolation, untrusted-input handling and citation restrictions remain.

No vendor establishes a universal requirement to include every adjacent fee. Sizing follows the owner's Fin reference; loading follows Lucid's existing shared chat.

Section9 single-recorder research requested by owner: cannot substantiate a universal industry standard requiring one active recorder. W3C Media Capture and Streams explicitly permits several tracks to represent the same source (https://www.w3.org/TR/mediacapture-streams/). Microsoft Copilot Chat documents an explicit start/stop dictation flow targeting its message composer (https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365-copilot/dictate-messages-to-microsoft-365-copilot-chat), but does not establish cross-composer exclusivity. One active recorder within Lucid is an engineering recommendation to prevent unintended simultaneous input, not a mandated standard; owner's acceptance was conditional on evidence, so do not silently treat it as unconditional implementation approval. ## Document summaries: broad-question correction and file explanations

Broad document questions now select bounded, corpus-scoped overview passages instead of requiring topical similarity. The short rehearsal document includes all its sections. Larger inputs use up to 24 passages spread across the available files/sections and disclose incomplete coverage. Exact filenames select their own document; an ambiguous singular request with multiple uploads asks which file. Harbor uses the same overview selection for its published seeded policies and never accesses private uploads. Generated facts retain the existing allowed-ID and source rendering boundaries.

Zero-byte files now receive an empty-file explanation. Whitespace-only plain text is identified as blank. PDFs/DOCX with no extractable text explain that the file may be blank or image-only and request a text-readable copy, without claiming OCR or a known image/blank distinction. Existing password, corrupt-file, resource-limit and unsupported-type handling remains. Named summary requests also explain missing, failed or processing files; all-file overviews say when non-ready uploads were excluded.

Focused retrieval/indexing checks passed 24 cases (116 assertions), followed by 51 affected summary/follow-up cases (439 assertions) after restricting coverage checks to summary turns. The first complete run exposed six mocked ordinary-follow-up regressions; their existing tests now pass without changed expectations. Live checks passed all three owner-style broad questions against the fictional rehearsal file (18 assertions), a named Harbor policy summary (four assertions), and an overview containing malicious document instructions (three assertions). Final composer test passed Pint, Rector, PHPStan and 921 tests (8,939 assertions), with three optional live-provider tests skipped, out of 924 total cases. No application code changed after this successful gate. No temporary scripts or diagnostics were introduced; the new fictional fixture and regression tests are retained. Herd site/Insights tools returned Transport closed, so no new Herd runtime performance evidence is claimed. The owner walkthrough should upload Desktop / Document-Chat-Test-Pack / 01-current-standard.md only, start with “Summarize the main points in this document,” then check numbered references against deposit/deadline and cancellation questions. The existing 05-whitespace-only.txt and 09-image-only.pdf cover the revised reading-error messages. Tone/length manual review remains pending. No owner files or workspaces were removed by this fix.

## Functionality follow-up: ticket limit in the regular browser

On November 13, Terry reported that his regular browser still hit the 30-ticket limit after the inactivity fix. He continued the UI walkthrough in a private window. A read-only database check found 30 tickets tied to one demo visit, created November 1–13. The session stores only its latest activity timestamp, so the prior idle periods cannot be reconstructed.

The current code checks for inactivity before refreshing a demo visit, and both limit messages omit the private-window workaround. The focused ticket and cleanup tests passed, but recovery in Terry's regular browser remains unverified. During functionality review, reproduce the cap with an existing browser session, trace which requests refresh activity and whether scheduled cleanup runs, and verify that the same browser can submit a ticket after the idle period. ## Inline feedback colors and lifetime — 2026-11-09

Shared local action feedback now uses green medium-weight text for successful actions, blue for progress/information, amber for warnings and red for errors. Routine Details saves, draft saves, internal-note saves, reply sends/rejections and standalone replacement resets hide after eight seconds. The shared component retains the text's layout space, so disappearance does not move controls. Timing pauses while hovered/focused or the document is hidden; timers and mutation observers clean up on removal. Independent action revision counters restart a confirmation even when the same action returns identical text. Dismissal is remembered on the owning Livewire DOM root so unrelated morphs or tab changes do not resurrect expired messages; navigation naturally releases this transient state. No timer server requests, vendor changes, toast overlay or extra application dependencies were added.

Processing messages, unchanged-draft explanations, regeneration/reset limits, replacement warnings, submission errors and the detailed queue-cleanup count stay visible until resolved/replaced. Queue cleanup is green but deliberately untimed because its detailed counts are useful information, not merely confirmation of the current form values. Permanent Sent/Confirmed result states remain. Existing viewport-level settings/copy toasts and deferred custom toast design remain separate.

Basis: [Zendesk Garden notification types](https://garden.zendesk.com/components/notifications/) distinguish success, info, warning and error. [W3C timing guidance](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/timing-adjustable) permits a temporary message when the result can still be determined elsewhere; actionable or otherwise unique information must remain available. Eight seconds is an application choice, not an industry-prescribed duration. [GOV.UK](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/notification-banner/) supports green expected-outcome confirmations but does not mandate an eight-second timeout.

Verification: 79 affected feature cases/673 assertions passed. Eight targeted browser cases passed: desktop/phone first/repeated save geometry (two cases/38 assertions), expiry/identical-resave/tab-return and hover pause plus persistent warning (three cases/22 assertions), queue reset (two cases/10 assertions), and Harbor save/send (one case/23 assertions). These verify actual green rendering, retained positions after timeout, fresh confirmation on repeated identical save, no resurrection, and warnings remaining after the success interval. Build, Pint and diff checks passed. Corrected one existing test-only queue badge expectation from obsolete 100 shades to the already-approved 50 shades; no ticket badge UI changed. Updated shared rules and repeatable walkthrough.

[GOV.UK notification guidance](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/notification-banner/) explicitly recommends placing directly task-relevant information within page content because banners can be missed. Intercom's public settings documentation describes saving but does not establish an exact confirmation-position mandate; do not claim all three products require identical UI placement. The local-feedback choice is this app's application of those principles.

## Captured Edge drift and removed the feedback loop — 2026-11-08

Read Terry's live local captures at2267×1096 (Edge90% zoom observed earlier). Window/document/other ancestors stayed still, while the middle panel changed from534px to roughly409–415px over2.5seconds, moving by about a pixel per animation frame. One return switch also moved372→0→515. These measurements are distinct from the earlier Chromium cases, which did not reproduce this Edge drift. The live behavior is consistent with the scroll listener repeatedly shrinking the root minimum height after small scroll-range corrections; fractional scaling is a likely trigger, not a separately proven universal cause.

Removed the scroll→height feedback path. Preserve the current visible bottom once on the reply-mode click, with upward rounding and a2px safety inset; release excess trailing space once after actual wheel/pointer/key input or resize. Ordinary scroll events no longer change the root height. Compact manual/AI editor dimensions, padding, drafts and focus remain. The earlier return-focus race is also corrected by using the visible-editor retry in both directions. Removed all temporary diagnostic click tracing and the local logging action after capture.

Ten reported-position cases passed across desktop,1080px window scrolling and scaled layouts, replaying partly visible first click → return → centered/edge click. Added a regression that a one-pixel browser scroll must not resize the preserved page; both scaled cases passed22 assertions. Final related workflow checks passed5 cases/234 assertions, sizing checks9 cases/211 assertions and affected feature suites28 cases/306 assertions. Build/Pint/diff checks passed. Shared rules and the walkthrough now prohibit this feedback pattern and require exact owner-position/scaling reproduction. Next: Terry refreshes and repeats his sequence to confirm Edge no longer creeps, then resume the ticket walkthrough. Owner visual acceptance remains pending; no claim that Chromium tests replace it. Heading review below remains complete and separate.

## Assessment label hierarchy and stable reply switching — 2026-11-08

Terry requested credible-source verification of label/value emphasis and View reasoning placement, and reported a jump when switching reply modes. GOV.UK's summary-list implementation uses bold keys with regular values (https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/summary-list/ and its official govuk-frontend summary-list Sass). Adopted this established option for the ticket's read-only summary: semibold Category/Priority/Sentiment labels, normal-weight values, retained priority urgency backgrounds, and View reasoning aligned with the value row. This is a project choice supported by an established design system, not a universal mandatory UI standard or a blanket change to all label/badge roles.

The previous reply modes had different content heights and ordinary focus() calls that can scroll the page (https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/HTMLElement/focus#parameters). Both modes now share a stable, shrinkable responsive grid slot; the manual editor uses its available writing space and scrolls internally for long text. Mode changes focus the active editor with preventScroll:true. Inactive panels retained for sizing are invisible, inert and aria-hidden; they cannot receive input or keyboard focus. AI draft256px sizing and other composer baselines remain; the internal manual area now fills this stable slot instead of the earlier short130px presentation. Both drafts are preserved. No animation or fixed page-height hack added.

## App-wide typography correction and consolidated follow-ups — 2026-11-07

Owner explicitly required comparing the entire approved journey (homepage, customer support and its states, documents, invoices, dashboard and agent ticket views), rather than copying one cited page's text size. The preceding isolated internal14px editor change was premature. This checkpoint supersedes the reopened-baseline note and settles shared text-entry styling. Complete correction list for this round, including the owner's added requests:
1. Centralized regular text-entry typography at16px across the application: all textareas, text/email/password/search/phone/URL/number/date inputs and selects. Textareas use1.5 line height. Covers both AI reply boxes, manual replies, chat, document/preview questions, notes/guidance, ticket creation, actual invoice-review fields and settings/auth fields. Shared chat/manual16px and native mobile form16px provide the existing approved baseline; compact14px body/labels/actions remain distinct roles. Preserved both256px suggested-reply boxes and existing composer/action dimensions.
2. Standardized showroom workspace main H1 titles at24px; ordinary dashboard, invoice-form and document-chat section headings at16px semibold, matching ticket sections. Preserved larger homepage and empty-state marketing headings and established navigation/guide typography.
3. Corrected the document composer controls to share the reserved microphone/send row, preventing the shared column layout from stacking each control on its own line.
4. Renamed Demo Harbor Save draft to Save edits. Verified it writes edited draft body to the existing private SuggestedReply record without sending or changing Awaiting review/Pending states. The original generated draft is already stored; this action saves subsequent edits. Approve and send also saves current edits as part of sending. Added matching guide explanation. No new storage location or autosave behavior.
5. Removed redundant review dividers before Customer request, AI assessment and Suggested reply in the internal ticket; retained spacing, navigation borders, Ticket information group divider and separators between list items. Last tool-list items finish open rather than adding trailing footer-like lines.
6. Checked internal ticket header against established ticket-workspace patterns. Kept the ticket subject as the H1 and existing reference/status/demo context; added a short instruction below it: Review the customer request, check the draft, and choose how to reply. This gives the same kind of orientation as other reviewed screens without repeating a generic large support-agent heading.
7. Recorded a standing shared rule: review the homepage, all previously reviewed areas and readiness history before choosing a comparison baseline; use computed styles by role and regression evidence. Never infer an app-wide standard from one cited screen. Report changes plainly.

References: Carbon's14px productive/16px expressive type system supports role-based sizing, not one mandatory size for all text: https://www.carbondesignsystem.com/building-blocks/foundations/typography/type-sets . Ticket ID/title conventions: https://www.intercom.com/help/en/articles/8300304-how-to-manage-customer-tickets . Exact app dimensions are project decisions.

## In-place Harbor reply modes and shared composers — 2026-11-07

Owner preferred keeping manual writing in the existing suggested-reply area on /demo/harbor, rather than opening another form or modal. Implemented an inline mode switch: Write your own reply hides the suggested reply/actions/source attribution and shows a blank manual editor in that same section. Back to suggested reply restores the edited AI draft; switching again preserves the manual draft. Neither mode switch sends or discards either draft. Existing approval and manual-send handlers remain unchanged. Sent state still shows history. Walkthrough now explains both paths and preservation.

Harbor chat and both manual editors share a130px initial composer surface,80px minimum text area,16px text insets, a reserved lower-right voice/send row, and matching40px circular send arrows. Editors grow as text increases; suggested-AI editor keeps its separate larger size. Full ticket manual modal remains a modal but is substantially wider (up to832px, viewport minus32px on narrow screens). Removed Use suggested reply from that modal; sending uses the in-box arrow with Send reply tooltip/accessibility label. Enter adds a newline in manual editors; Ctrl/Command+Enter sends. Dictation never sends automatically. On phones, view tabs wrap as whole labels into additional rows rather than overflowing their36px rows.

Guidance supporting the in-place composer choice and independent draft preservation: https://www.intercom.com/help/en/articles/8587194-how-to-use-copilot ; https://www.intercom.com/changes/en/138906-never-lose-a-draft-when-switching-between-replies-and-notes ; https://v10.carbondesignsystem.com/community/patterns/create-flows/ . Exact dimensions and mode controls are our design choices, not mandated vendor copies.

Owner authorized reliable-source verification, consistent compact regular actions, regression tests, and a complete review list. ## Duplicate instruction scrollbar arrow correction — 2026-11-06

Investigated the owner's purple mark at the scrollbar end. Strengthened the shared guide override to hide those duplicate native buttons. Retained the thin rounded lavender thumb, hover color, proportional thumb length, and continuous-hold controls. No spacing or wording changes.

Build and two desktop browser cases passed (82 assertions), including native-button hiding, custom arrows, hold/release/end behavior and layout. Compound scrollbar pseudo-elements are not exposed by Chromium's computed-style API; coverage checks the generic computed button style plus the loaded specific override. Owner visual confirmation of the reported mark remains pending. No deployment.

## Future capability scope from the owner walkthrough — 2026-10-31

This is a planning backlog, not an implemented-capability claim or authorization to expand package 3. Finish the current UI/client-journey walkthrough first. The owner wants commercially significant document/data automation rather than a three-line extraction demonstration. Existing functionality and UI are adjustable; select supported formats, volumes and acceptance targets against relevant job specifications before expanding them. Do not promise hundreds of items until measured and tested. A small sample proves the process only.

Research source: [20 saved Upwork job summaries](../../../.codex/visualizations/2026/10/29/01a18501-a6d7-7af2-a563-51d78cc66b88/upwork-job-research.md) (absolute source: C:/Users/trrla/.codex/visualizations/2026/10/29/01a18501-a6d7-7af2-a563-51d78cc66b88/upwork-job-research.md). These are historical job postings, not client reviews or verified current opportunities. The research file's four-entry recommendation is superseded by the approved three-demo navigation; replacements belong inside support.

Candidate evidence to build and verify against that research:

- Support (jobs 7–10): grounded answers, inspectable policies, fictional order lookup/replacement, explicit human escalation with conversation context, staff correction/approval and ticket tracking. Broader returns/refunds, automatic escalation rules and real store/helpdesk connectors are separate scope; existing replacement tools do not establish them.
- Documents (jobs 12–15): sample and own-file inputs, answers with source passages, multi-document retrieval, summaries/structured extraction where useful, unsupported-question handling and measured answer quality. Define required file formats, document/page counts, tables and document lengths before implementation/acceptance.
- Invoice/data extraction (jobs 11–13): detailed multipage invoices, many line items, repeated/batch processing where the selected job needs it, reliable table/field extraction, missing/uncertain-field flags, arithmetic validation, corrections/confirmation and spreadsheet-ready exports. Evaluate a realistic 20–30-line sample and a separately measured large sample; counts are proposals, not accepted capacity limits. Invoice intake currently accepts selectable-text PDFs, not scanned/photo OCR. Evaluate OCR, photos, spreadsheets and other formats only as requirements justify them.
- Downstream use: extracting PDF contents into rows/columns for spreadsheets, accounting or Python/JupyterLab analysis is the intended business outcome. Seaborn visualization/analytics comes after extraction; no Python/Anaconda service or analytics dashboard is selected or implemented by this discussion.
- Integrations/controlled work (jobs 1–6, 13, 15): validated inputs, duplicate prevention, queued work, recoverable failures, permissions, human approvals and traceable results; prove a real CRM/accounting/store connector if a proposal depends on it. Current showroom patterns do not establish HubSpot, Salesforce, n8n, GoHighLevel, Gorgias or WooCommerce production experience.
- CRM/calendar, telephone agents, video production and SEO/outreach automation (jobs 1–6, 16–20) remain separate future opportunities, not extra promises attached to these three demos. Dictation is not a phone agent; site SEO is not an SEO automation product.

Sequence: finish package 3 UI/walkthrough; then re-read the relevant original job requirements and select a focused functionality expansion (package number not assigned). Package 4 retains AI quality/tone acceptance and package 5 performance/operations verification. Add tests for selected functionality and establish pages/items/files per run, accuracy, elapsed time, concurrency, cost and failure/retry expectations before calling larger-scale use ready. Broader capability work is not automatically covered by the existing performance package.

Walkthrough position: Start here support and document explanation steps were accepted by the owner. Invoice explanation prompted this scope discussion; resume there, then continue the last introduction step. Homepage support card and tour now explicitly say hand off to a human; 24 affected homepage checks/409 assertions passed on that copy follow-up. Keep current UI wording truthful while leaving room to revise it with future functionality.

Broader production recovery, provider retention/billing and off-peak capacity checks are not closed by this deployment. No full-showroom-readiness claim is made.

Do not treat a local Feature pass as Forge verification. Do not treat a Forge deploy as readiness to show clients.

## How to use the tables

**Status** is `Closed`, `Local closed`, `Open`, or `Out of scope`. Open items name who or what they need. They are **not** passed.

**Blocks:** `Landing` (commit/push), `Verify` (release verification after deploy), `Client` (walkthrough), or `No`.

## Replacement Confirm (why this is not a call-ID swap)

The plan said persist the provider `call_id` (`call_…` / `ToolCall.resultId`) and resume OpenAI with `Decisions::approve()`. Stage 4 already showed that was the wrong fix.

**What broke.** Confirm saved the Harbor request in PHP, then resumed the stored conversation. Laravel/AI `PendingApproval.id` is the tool-call `fc_…`. OpenAI `function_call_output` wants `call_…`. The live 400 was worse than that namespace mix-up: stored pauses replay **reasoning** blocks without the matching `function_call` items, so OpenAI returned `No tool call found for function call output` (Stage 4 used LookupOrder’s `call_AGgXr…`). Ticket **HR-C09B3B** was already saved; `ai_runs` 663 failed.

**What shipped.** Confirm and cancel no longer call OpenAI. `executeVisitorDecision` commits or cancels locally, writes the assistant line, completes the `AiRun`, and deletes the SDK conversation so a later turn cannot replay the broken pause. `storeProposal` still records `snapshot.provider_call_id` when the model returns one; Confirm does not send it.

**Verified**

- Feature `confirming a proposal saves a request without resuming OpenAI tool output`: `Http::preventStrayRequests()` + `Http::fake()`, `beginConfirm()`, `HR-…` request, proposal `confirmed`, `ai_runs` `completed` with `error` null, assistant body contains the reference, `conversation_id` null, `Http::assertNothingSent()`. Feature cancel test still present (`cancel saves nothing`).
- Live Herd 2026-10-26 17:13:32, new case: **HR-5EFB44**, proposal `confirmed`, `tool_call_id` still `fc_050b98ef…`, snapshot `provider_call_id` `call_Wq9L833JvQPA0zGKaAXBkOcJ`, `ai_runs.id` **671** `completed`, payload `decision: approve`, `error` null.
- Live Forge after `d93755f`: new case Confirm **HR-5A6717**, “Your replacement request is saved. Reference HR-5A6717. Nothing is shipped.” No 400. Cancel on a later case: activity and conversation **Nothing was saved.**, no new HR, no 400.

## 2026-10-28 checkpoint verification

The tables above retain the 2026-10-26 handoff evidence; current CI/deployment state is authoritative in GitHub Actions and Forge deployment history. Local `composer test` now passes: Pint, Rector dry-run, Larastan, 430 passed / 1 skipped, 3340 assertions. `npm run build` passed. All nine deployment-gate scenarios passed. Browser tests now use built assets instead of `public/hot`, preventing the development server from reloading pages during tests. No assertions were weakened or tests removed.

Forge push-to-deploy is verified off, and the installed deploy script matches `.github/forge-deploy.sh`. The main push must pass the tests job before its deploy job can invoke Forge; Forge independently checks the exact SHA and CI result. The deployment-hook response only acknowledges submission, so completion must also be checked in Forge and the deployed SHA verified over SSH.

Pre-push SSH confirmed the old release `2d6943d`, Lucid Octane `--workers=1`, and Lucid queue worker running. CareerForge worker `worker-987181` is running again (all three processes had matching uptime), consistent with the documented reboot autostart. Its earlier pause was not permanent; no worker settings were changed in this checkpoint. Device/upload/microphone checks remain open. The broad system-design audit and complete Lucid naming migration are follow-on work after this verified checkpoint.
